**Question.** Can the effectiveness be increased even further?

**Answer.** Only up to the number 9. <note>In many alchemical and mystical traditions, 9 represents the threshold of completion, while 10 represents the return to Unity or the Divine.</note>

**Question.** When is it made into the **means of revelation**?

**Answer.** When it has circled the **Wheel of Nature** ten times and burns without being consumed, when it has been ignited by the heat of the **sun**, and coagulates through the cold of the **moonlight**. <note>The "Wheel of Nature" (original: *Rad der Natur*) is a concept often found in the works of Jacob Boehme, referring to the cyclical movement of the seven properties of eternal nature.</note>

**Question.** What does it actually mean to pass through the **Wheel of Nature** ten times?

**Answer.** The doubled **light-power** must pass through the seven circuits ten times by means of **fermentations**. <note>original: "Gährungen." In alchemy, fermentation is the stage where the "medicine" is given the power to transform or "leaven" other substances, increasing its potency through repeated cycles.</note>

**Question.** How is it ignited by the heat of the **sun**?

**Answer.** As soon as it has become a **fire-red stone** after ten cycles, it is dissolved by a heat equivalent to that of the month of **July**, and transformed into a **watery fire** and a **fiery water**. <note>original: "wässrigen Feuer und feurigen Wasser." This is a classic alchemical paradox describing the "Universal Solvent," a substance that possesses the opposing qualities of both moisture and heat simultaneously.</note>

**Question.** How can it coagulate through the cold of the **moon**, or what is this technical language meant to convey?

**Answer.** When the **double vessel** is opened and the activity of the heat is restrained, it attains its density.

**Question.** How can one penetrate all properties of the **primordial power** <note>original: "Urkraft."</note> with the help of this doubled **light-power**?

**Answer.** From the **Great Stone**, three

## Catechism of the S. Masters.

---

**Q.** Are you a Scottish Master?
**A.** Yes! I have the honor to be one.

<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism for the Scottish Master degree. Following the previous page’s visual description of the tracing board symbols—such as the Table of Showbread and Noah’s Ark—this section transitions into a formal Q&A (catechism) regarding the candidate's symbolic initiation among the ruins of the Temple and the spiritual meaning of the Tabernacle's furniture.</meta>

Q. Where were you received?
A. Upon the ruins of the Temple.
Q. By what means?
A. Through four **accolades** <term>Accolades: Ritual taps with a sword on the candidate's shoulders, used in high-grade or "chivalric" Masonic degrees to signify the transition to a new rank.</term>.
Q. What did you behold when you searched the holy places? —
A. Ruins of the steps, of the columns, and of the door of the T. <note>original: "T.," referring to the Temple of Solomon.</note>
Q. What did you find as you searched?
A. Four golden plates upon which the great name of the Immortal J. <note>original: "J.," representing the Tetragrammaton or Jehovah.</note> was engraved.
Q. What did you do when you found these?
A. I fell upon my knees, adored him in spirit, and praised him.
Q. What does the **Altar of Incense** signify?
A. The fiery prayers and the fervent gratitude of the ancient S. S. M. M. <note>S. S. M. M.: Likely an abbreviation for "Schottische Super-Maurer-Meister" or "Souveräne Schottische Maurer-Meister" (Scottish Master Masons).</note> upon the discovery of the four golden plates, which rise like fragrant incense to J.
Q. What does the **Brazen Sea** <term>Brazen Sea: A large bronze basin described in the Hebrew Bible that stood in the inner court of Solomon's Temple, used for the ritual purification of priests.</term> signify?
A. It points to the purification of a **St. John’s Master** <note>original: "Johanniter Meister." In Masonry, this refers to a Master Mason of the "Blue Lodge" or "St. John's Lodge" (the first three degrees).</note> from all vices, without which he cannot enter into the Sanctuary of the S. S. M. M.
Q. What does the **Ark of the Covenant** signify?
A. It reminds us of the silent adoration of the great J. and of the union with him.

<summary>This page contains a Masonic catechism explaining the symbolic transition from a St. John's Master to a Scottish Master, emphasizing the discovery of the divine name among the Temple ruins. It interprets the Altar of Incense, the Brazen Sea, and the Ark of the Covenant as symbols of prayer, purification, and divine union.</summary>
<keywords>Scottish Master, Temple of Solomon, Accolades, Altar of Incense, Brazen Sea, Ark of the Covenant, Masonic Catechism, Ritual Purification</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism for the Scottish Master degree. It provides symbolic interpretations for various biblical artifacts and stories, linking them to the moral duties and spiritual goals of the initiate. The text moves from the furniture of the Tabernacle to historical-mythic symbols like the Tower of Babel and Noah's Ark, and finally revisits the foundational vocabulary of the earlier Apprentice degree.</meta>

Q. What does the **candlestick with seven lights** signify?

A. The seven powers of the soul and the sevenfold light of our Order.

Q. What does the **Table of Showbread** <term>Table of Showbread: A golden table described in the Bible (Exodus 25) that stood in the Tabernacle to hold twelve loaves of bread, representing the presence of God among the twelve tribes.</term> signify?

A. It signifies the nourishment of our spirit which we receive through the great morality of our Order.

Q. What does the **letter G** in the **Blazing Star** signify?

A. The Greatness, the Splendor of God, or the Glory of the great J. <note>original: "J.," standing for Jehovah or Jah.</note>

Q. What does the **Tower of Babel** signify?

A. It reminds us of the haughtiness and pride of those people whom God punished with the confusion of tongues and scattering throughout the whole world—vices which a **Scottish Master** <term>Scottish Master: A high-ranking degree in Freemasonry that focuses on the preservation of sacred knowledge and the spiritual rebuilding of the Temple.</term> should flee and avoid with all earnestness.

Q. What does **Noah’s Ark** signify?

A. Just as the wickedness of the first humans was punished by the Deluge, and only seven souls <note>The text specifies seven (original: "sieben"), though Genesis typically lists eight survivors. This number likely mirrors the "seven powers of the soul" mentioned above.</note> were preserved within it, so should we apply the powers of our souls so that we may be found blameless in all our actions in the eyes of the great J., just as those souls were.

Q. What does the word **Jachin**, which you already came to know as an **Apprentice**, signify?

A. It means firm and steadfast.

Q. What does this signify?

Answer. Regarding the Δ and □.

Q. What do these signify?

A. The triangle [Δ] signifies the three lower degrees; the square [□] signifies the fourth degree or the **Scottish Master Degree**.

Q. Who illuminates your Lodge [☒]?

A. The **Blazing Star** with its G, or the glory of the Great J. <note>The letter "G" in Masonry often stands for Geometry or God; the "J" likely refers to the Tetragrammaton or Jehovah, representing the divine presence.</note>

Q. How many lights do you have in the Lodge [☒]?

A. Four.

Q. What do they signify?

A. The four **Sublime Scottish Masters** <note>original: "S. S. M. M." (Sublimen Schottischen Meister).</note> who found the four golden plates beneath the **ruins of the Temple**.

After the instruction has been given, the laws are read out, as is the protocol, and they are signed. Then the **Sublime Master** <term>Sublime Master: The presiding officer of the Scottish Lodge.</term> inquires through his wardens whether anyone has anything to propose for the welfare of the Lodge [☒]. Then the Lodge [☒] is closed again by <note>v v — — represents a specific rhythmic pattern of knocks (battery) used to signal the end of the meeting.</note>.

The clothing in this degree must be green. <note>In this branch of Masonry, green replaces the traditional blue of the lower degrees, symbolizing hope and the vital force of the order.</note> The knocks and all ritual movements <note>original: "Tempos," referring to the synchronized gestures used during the ritual meal.</note> at the table must be done by four, just as with the Masters <note>original: "Mstr." (Meister)</note> everything is done by three. After an initiation, the proceedings are always concluded with a magnificent banquet. **St. Andrew's Day** is a very solemn feast for us. <term>St. Andrew's Day: Observed on November 30th. St. Andrew is the patron saint of Scotland, and this day remains a primary feast day for Scottish Rite Masonry.</term>

<meta>This page transitions from the ritual instructions of the Scottish Master degree to a historical narrative (or "legend of the grade"). ### History of the Scottish Masters and Knight-Novices of Saint Andrew.

<note>original: "R. N." stands for Ritter-Novizen (Knight-Novices), a transitional rank within this specific Masonic system.</note>

The most ancient warriors who possessed the form of a disciplined corps—the **Knights of the Dawn** and of **Palestine**, the ancestors and fathers of the **M[asons]** <note>The original text uses the abbreviation "M . . .", a common practice in Masonic documents to maintain secrecy regarding the word "Masons."</note>—these enlightened and sublime men, whose point of origin I am not permitted to name, had looked upon all the humiliations which the **Kingdom of Judah** suffered with sorrow. For a long time, they had hoped that God would finally deign to cast a merciful glance upon this **Holy Land**, where His presence had already been revealed since the giving of the Law. <note>The "giving of the Law" refers to the receiving of the Ten Commandments on Mount Sinai.</note>

To most, it was still unknown whether His mysterious and divine birth had recently sanctified these places through the benefit of the **Law of Grace**. <note>The "Law of Grace" is a theological term referring to the Christian era and the teachings of the New Testament, following the "Law of Works" of the Old Testament.</note> Scattered in various remote regions where unfortunate events and the almost total downfall of the Jewish people had cast them, they awaited a fortunate change. This change might restore them to the inheritance of their fathers and provide them with the means to **restore the Temple for the third time**, <term>Third Temple: In this tradition, the first temple was built by Solomon and the second by Zerubbabel. The "third restoration" often refers to a spiritual or symbolic rebuilding by the knightly orders.</term> to resume their offices within it, and to regain, under a peaceful government, the splendor and positions of honor which they had always previously held. By virtue of these positions, they were always close to the sacred person of their sovereign

It frames the order's origins within the transition from the Old Testament Law to the Christian Law of Grace.</summary>

sacrifice to praise, whose supernatural self-sacrifice—still held in fresh memory—destroyed the realm of coarse superstition and introduced in its place true worship out of pure love. <meta>This page continues the speaker's address to the Order. ...who we have been, and what are we now? We existed under the protection of virtue; we lived with our friends, our fellow citizens, our Dear Brothers <note>original: "B. B.", abbreviation for "Brüder" (Brothers), a standard fraternal greeting.</note>. These great guilds are now merely a shadow of what they once were. It is the fate of all human institutions to decay imperceptibly and, as the years pass, to lean toward their ruin; the destructive hand of time shows itself just as much in the Order as it does in the world.

Let us cast away these sad images, My Dear Brothers <note>original: "M. B. B.", abbreviation for "Meine lieben Brüder" (My Dear Brothers).</note>. Live happily—not through the possession of riches and goods, but through the inner testimony of your conscience. Live happily! Not through honor and high office, nor through the splendor of birth, but through your morals and integrity. The heart—the kind-hearted soul, the heart of the Scots <note>In this context, "Scots" (Schotten) refers to practitioners of "Scottish" masonry, a specific branch or high degree of the movement that claimed ancient lineage.</note>—let that be your treasure. A wretched man stands before us; he pleads with us for our help; if our hearts close themselves, then we are no longer Scots.

Such was not the conduct of the holy man <note>Likely referring to a patron saint of the Order, such as Saint Andrew (patron of Scotland) or Saint John the Almoner.</note> whom we have chosen as our father. Born poor, he devoted his service to the needy; without reproach, he gained Brothers through his zeal; without friends, he attracted imitators through his virtue. Let the deeds of great men be carved in bronze and marble. Of our patron saint, it will be said: He loved his brothers.

<meta>Following the title page for the Sixth Degree (Provincial Capitular of the Red Cross), this page provides the cryptographic key—a substitution cipher—necessary for members to encode and decode the secret communications, passwords, and rituals associated with this rank.</meta>

# C i p h e r . <note>original: "Chiffre." In a Masonic context, a "cipher" is a system of secret writing where symbols or modified letters replace the standard alphabet to protect the order's secrets from the uninitiated.</note>

<image-desc>A handwritten substitution cipher key. At the top of the page, the title "Cipher" is written in a spaced-out serif script. Below this title are two groupings of cipher symbols paired with their corresponding alphabet letters. 

The first grouping consists of a row of fourteen unique symbols—including a backwards 'C', various geometric shapes (triangles, squares, circles), and symbols resembling Greek letters like Pi and Gamma—positioned above the letters: a b c d e f g h i k l m n o.

The second grouping consists of symbols positioned above the letters: p q r s t u v w x ÿ z. These symbols include an infinity sign (over 'p'), a triangle with a horizontal bar (over 'r'), a triangle with a vertical bar (over 's'), a digit-like '2' (over 't'), a square with a bracket on top (over 'u'), a '6'-like shape (over 'v'), and a hook (over 'w'). A complex, flourished symbol is placed above the letter 'z'. The spaces above 'q', 'x', and 'ÿ' remain blank. The letter 'y' in the alphabet row is written with a trema (ÿ).</image-desc>

->a b c d e f g h i k l m n o<-

->p q r s t u v w x ÿ z<- <note>The use of "ÿ" (y with a diaeresis) was common in older French and Germanic manuscripts, often used interchangeably with "ij" or to indicate a specific vowel sound that has since been standardized.</note>

<summary>This page presents the secret alphabet or substitution cipher for the Sixth Degree. It consists of geometric and pseudo-Greek symbols corresponding to the Latin alphabet, used to secure the degree's sensitive information.</summary>

# Sixth Degree.

### Concerning the True Working Masonry, or that of a Provincial Capitulary of the Red Cross.

---

<image-desc>A block of seven lines of text written in a secret Masonic or Rosicrucian cipher. The characters consist of geometric angles, boxes with dots, circles with crosses, and other symbolic glyphs arranged in justified lines.</image-desc>

### Arrangement and Decoration of the Hall.

1) Behind the seat of the <term>Provincial<note>The presiding officer or high-ranking leader of a specific geographic province within the order.</note></term>, there is on the wall a tapestry two <term>feet<note>original: "Schuh" (Shoe) — a historical unit of measurement approximately equal to a modern foot.</note></term> long and two feet wide made of white silver-moire <note>A silk fabric with a wavy, "watered" appearance, highly prized for its reflective qualities.</note>, upon which this <term>kabbalistic figure<note>Referring to the cipher shown in the image above, which likely encodes a sacred name or mystical concept.</note></term> must be embroidered, and the Chapter Cross must be attached below it.

<summary>This page introduces the "Sixth Degree" of the order, specifically for Provincial Capitularies of the Red Cross, and begins detailing the ritual decorations of the lodge hall, including a silver tapestry with a secret cipher.</summary>
<keywords>Sixth Degree, Red Cross, Freemasonry, Rosicrucian, Provincial Capitulary, ritual decoration, silver-moire, Kabbalistic figure, Chapter Cross</keywords>

--- Page 198 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for the ritual space of the Sixth Degree, focusing on the furniture and altar arrangement. It follows the description of the silver-moire tapestry on page 177.</meta>

2) The <term>canopy<note>original: "Baldachin" — a ceremonial fabric covering suspended over an important object or person.</note></term>, which must be fastened above the previously mentioned tapestry, is made of red <term>taffeta<note>A high-end, crisp silk fabric.</note></term> trimmed with golden braids and fringes. On top stands a sun made of gilded brass; on the front of the <term>cornice<note>original: "Kranz" — literally a wreath or crown, referring here to the decorative border or valance of the canopy.</note></term>, the kabbalistic figure is embroidered. *See Fig. 3.* <note>original: "Vide Fig. 3."</note>

3) Under the canopy, behind the altar, stands a large armchair covered in <term>poppy-red<note>original: "ponceau-rothem" — a vivid, deep scarlet color named after the corn poppy.</note></term> velvet, trimmed with golden braids and fringes; the frame must be of gilded woodcarving.

4) The altar must be four <term>feet<note>original: "Schuh" (Shoe) — a historical unit of measurement approximately equal to a modern foot.</note></term> long, three feet high, and two feet wide. The molding and ornamentation are richly gilded, and its front panel is divided into thirteen squares. Of these, the top and bottom four are light blue; of the middle ones, the two outermost are green, the following two are white, and the center one must be <term>fire-colored<note>original: "Feuerfarb" — a brilliant, intense reddish-orange.</note></term>. Upon the top is laid a white silk cloth trimmed with golden braids and fringes; however, it must be no longer or wider than necessary for the golden fringes to hang just over the top plate. On the altar, to the right and left, stand two two-branched candlesticks. Between them lies a <term>command staff<note>original: "Comando-Staab" — a ceremonial wand or mace indicating authority.</note></term> covered in red velvet, the upper part of which is decorated with a gilded sun, and the bottom of which must be fitted with a <term>ferrule<note>original: "Zwinge" — a metal cap or ring used to strengthen or protect the end of a wooden staff.</note></term> 1½ inches wide.

### Clothing of the Provincial, the Priors, Officials, and Capitulars.

---

a) The Provincial wears upon his body a <term>plastron<note>original: "Plastron" — a decorative chest-piece or breastplate.</note></term> of white <term>silver moire<note>original: "Silber-Moor" — a heavy silk fabric with a "watered" or wavy appearance, woven here with silver threads.</note></term> trimmed with golden fringes; over this, he wears a long, light-blue <term>choir robe<note>original: "Chor-Rock"</note></term> with wide sleeves and trimmed with golden lace. Upon his head, he wears a <term>mitre<note>original: "Mytra"</note></term> or cap, fashioned almost like a Turkish turban, except that it widens further at the top and is secured with a light-blue velvet band, which is embroidered with gold and features a sun embroidered on the front. Beneath the choir robe, he wears his sword over a white silver-moire <term>sword belt<note>original: "Wehrgehänge"</note></term> richly adorned with gold. Around his neck, hanging down to the chest on a poppy-red, three-finger-wide ribbon—which is threaded on both outer sides with 12 gold threads—is the <term>Order Cross<note>original: "Ordens-Kreuz"</note></term>. In his right hand, while the Chapter is in session, he holds the <term>baton of command<note>original: "Commandostab"</note></term>, and in the left, the <term>orb<note>original: "Kreuzapfel" — literally "cross-apple," referring to a globus cruciger, a symbol of authority.</note></term>; he wears boots and gilded spurs.

## Dress of the Provincial, the Priors, Officers,
## and Capitulars.

---

a) The <term>Provincial<note>The high-ranking regional leader of the Order.</note></term> wears upon his body a <term>plastron<note>original: "Plastron" — a decorative chest-piece or breastplate worn over the shirt.</note></term> made of white silver <term>moiré<note>original: "Silber-Moor" — a silk fabric with a wavy, watered appearance, often used in high-status regalia.</note></term> trimmed with gold fringes; over this, a long, light-blue <term>choir robe<note>original: "Chor-Rock" — a ceremonial gown or surplice.</note></term> with wide sleeves and trimmed with gold lace. On his head, he wears a <term>mitre<note>original: "Mytra"</note></term> or cap, shaped almost like a <term>turban<note>original: "türkischer Bund" — literally "Turkish alliance" or "Turkish knot," a common 18th-century term for a turban-style headpiece.</note></term>, except that it flares wider at the top and is secured with a light-blue velvet band, which is embroidered with gold and features a sun embroidered on the front. Under the choir robe, he wears his sword over a white silver-moiré <term>scabbard-belt<note>original: "Wehrgehänge"</note></term> richly adorned with gold. Around his neck, hanging down to the breast on a <term>poppy-red<note>original: "ponceaurothen" — a vivid scarlet color.</note></term> ribbon three fingers wide (which is woven with twelve gold threads on both outer edges), he wears the <term>Cross of the Order<note>original: "Ordens-Kreuz"</note></term>. In his right hand, while the Chapter is open, he holds the <term>command staff<note>original: "Commandostab" — a baton of office.</note></term>, and in the left the <term>orb<note>original: "Kreuzapfel" — literally "cross-apple," a globus cruciger symbolizing authority.</note></term>; he wears boots and gilded spurs.

### Opening of a Prefecture Chapter.
---

The Chapter consists of the Provincial, two Priors, one Provincial Councilor, one Treasurer, one Orator, and one Almoner who is simultaneously the <term>Steward<note>original: "Oeconomus" — an officer in charge of finances or household management.</note></term>.

,,When a <term>Chapter<note>original: "Capitul" — a formal assembly or governing body within the Order.</note></term> has been convened by the Provincial Grand Master, the <term>Brothers<note>original: "B. „...two side-panels. The <term>Provincial<note>original: „Prov.“ — the presiding officer of a territorial division within this secret order.</note></term>: Give me the word.

Provincial: Is it the right time for the opening?

Answer: It is the same hour of the great day which the Almighty chose for the beginning of time.

Provincial: How old are you?

Answer: My age is beyond counting.

## Initiation Rituals

---

The **Provincial** speaks, after all previously observed opening rituals: It is known to you, enlightened and right-worthy superiors and members of this regular and perfect **Chapter**, that by the departure of <term>N. N.<note>original: „N. N.“ — a Latin abbreviation for 'Nomen Nominandum,' used as a placeholder for the name of the departed member to be spoken during the ceremony.</note></term> the full complement of our Provincial Chapter has been diminished; however, according to age and merit, the Sublime and Right-Worthy **Past Master** and **Knight of Saint Andrew** has been proposed to take his place, was unanimously accepted, and has been found capable in his examination to be a member of this enlightened Chapter. I am therefore willing at this hour to reward his zeal, loyalty, diligence, and fear of God through further advancement, provided that from the time of his last examination until today no event has occurred that could hinder our intention.

Hereupon the **Provincial** pauses for several seconds; the two **Priors** inquire among their <term>columns<note>original: „Colonnen“ — in Masonic and chivalric orders, this refers to the two rows of members seated along the north and south walls of the lodge room.</note></term> one after the other. If a significant objection is found, the promotion must remain suspended; if no obstacle is found, all <term>Brothers<note>original: „B. B.“ — abbreviation for 'Brüder' (Brothers).</note></term> cry aloud: **He is worthy of this advancement!** Now the **Provincial** dispatches the **Treasurer**, who goes to the <term>candidate<note>original: „Aspiranten“</note></term> in the preparation room, [to collect from] him the stipulated fee for clothing, the Order’s Cross, the diploma, and initiation—

Then the **candidate** is led into the <term>West<note>original: „Abend“ (Evening) — in Masonic and knightly rituals, the "Evening" or West represents the entrance to the lodge, opposite the "Morning" or East where the leadership sits.</note></term>, placed between both **Priors**, whereupon the doors are locked and the **Capitulars** <note>Members of the Chapter.</note> station themselves inward at the tables, so that the two **Priors** stand at the bottom, and at the top, to the right, stand the **Provincial Councilor**, the **Orator**, and two **Capitulars**, and to the left, the **Treasurer**, the **Almoner**, and three **Capitulars**. The **Provincial** speaks: “Illustrious and Most Reverend <term>Knights and Brothers<note>original: „RRr. und BB.“ — abbreviations for Ritter and Brüder.</note></term>: **Under-Prior**, show the sublime and very venerable Brother **Past Master** how he shall approach the presiding seat of this most sublime **Chapter**.”

The **Under-Prior** demonstrates it: namely, he steps between the two <term>seven-branched candlesticks<note>original: „siebenarmigten Leuchter“ — symbolic of the Menorah, often used in high-degree rituals to represent spiritual light.</note></term>, sets his feet <term>at a right angle<note>original: „winkelförmig“ — a traditional ritual stance where the heels are joined to form a square.</note></term>, crosses both arms over his chest so that the right hand lies on the left breast and the left hand lies below it on the right breast, bows deeply to the earth, takes one small step forward, repeats this expression of reverence a second time, and then takes two steps toward the **Prefect** or **Provincial** and performs the sign of reverence for the last time. Then he makes the sign, namely that of a <term>Contemplative Priest<note>original: „anschauenden Priesters“ — refer to page 196; this refers to a specific rank within the Order focused on mystical vision.</note></term>. Once the **candidate** has approached in this manner according to the instructions given, the **Provincial** has him take the vow of the strictest secrecy and steadfastness in the following way.

# Seventh Degree.

---

->Concerning true operative Masonry or the degree of the Magus, Magi, the Knights of Clarity and Light (*).<-

---

### No. I.

Holy Scripture contains very remarkable and sublime concepts regarding humankind: among the privileges God has granted him, this is the first—that he was not made from coarse earth, as other animals were, but from a substance that bore the <term>signature: a mark or essence that reveals an essence's divine origin or purpose</term> of the Deity itself; therefore he

<note>(*) In this degree, neither aprons nor swords, nor any other ceremonies involving a <term>Tracing Board: a ritual carpet or floor cloth (Tapis) depicting the symbols of the degree</term> take place. The above is the sum of what is partially known, and it indeed provides full disclosure. Regarding <term>spirit-seeing: the practice of communing with or summoning spirits</term> in this degree, I cannot possibly permit everything to be printed here; however, I will provide this explanation: through the excitation of the imagination and the use of optical instruments, some claim to show actual spirits. The Order itself, however, says: "My brother, pray to the <term>Great Architect: a common Masonic term for God as the creator of the universe</term> for the inner light within you; without this and the Great Architect’s grace, all the teachings of the Order are ineffective and dead."</note>

...most of the <term>Universal Spirit: the vital, animating force believed to permeate the entire universe</term>, unquestionably within himself, and is well-suited to be called the Center of the World. But one should not understand this as the human microcosm, but rather as the microcosm, the son of the microcosm <note>original: "microcosmum microcosmi filium"</note>. And this applies solely to this matter, which is begotten from the entire interconnectedness of nature and, even more so, is animated by a higher power. <term>Basil: likely Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist</term> and <term>Bernard: likely Bernard of Treves, a 15th-century Italian alchemist</term> do not speak of the raw subject <note>original: "subjecto forum crudo"</note>, but rather insofar as it has already attained a certain degree of labor. In all things in the world, the <term>First Matter: the original, chaotic substance from which all things are created</term> is contained; for if this were not so, they could not have been generated at all. And since a constant creation proceeds in the world, nature cannot do without it for even a moment. In man, more of this is contained than is found in all other creatures. If one could now extract such a substance, one would also have to be able to extract it from all other things. For all things, in common chemical preparation, yield a <term>Salt: ⊖, the physical principle of body and stability in alchemy</term> and, through reduction, agree with one another in their origin. But by what feat of art should one perform this extraction? (For the <term>Philosophical Work: the alchemical process of spiritual and material transformation</term> is quite different from the work of extracting; the former begins solely with the matter itself, which is treated in a small glass vessel just as nature itself does on a large scale.)

It is an established fact that from mere <term>particulars: <note>original: "Particularibus"</note> specific, individual types of matter or species</term> in nature, no <term>universal: <note>original: "Universale"</note> a single underlying essence that contains the potential of all things</term> can be produced, even if one were to mix them together ever so artfully. He who sows barley will harvest barley, and not wheat, oats, millet, and so forth. For a work which is to contain all things within itself, a substance must be chosen that is not specific to any <term>particulars</term>, and yet contains everything necessary for the generation of all <term>particulars</term> within that **Universal**. Even if you take a seed from minerals, metals, vegetables, animals, humans, and so forth, and—if it were possible—combine them in a certain proportion, it is still no **Universal**; rather, it is a patched-together thing from which something new is just as unlikely to arise as a complete man is from the severed parts of a human being. <meta>This page continues the catalog of names for the "First Matter" (Prima Materia). While the previous page focused on "Chaos," this text expands into classical, Paracelsian, and naturalistic descriptors, explaining why the Matter is compared to sticky substances like amber or gum.</meta>

Another name <note>The source text reads "Mann" (man), likely a scribal error for "Nahme" (name)</note> is Hyle, that is, *Matter from which something is made* <note>original Latin: "Materia ex quo aliquid fit"</note>; the ancients called it Pan, that is, "All," from which everything can become. They also called it Proteus the Firstborn. They call it Gum, for it truly looks like a gum and is sticky, which is why it is also called *viscous moisture* <note>original Latin: "viscosa humidata"</note>. Paracelsus <note>The famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note> calls it Electrum, or Amber, and I might say, perhaps too clearly: for Electrum has its origin here, and one also finds flies, gnats, and spiders within amber—proof that the Matter of the Electrum was initially fluid or sticky, such that these little creatures became stuck in it. The name Ambergris <note>original: "Ambra"</note> has the same origins. Furthermore, they call it *Water of Life* <note>original Latin: "Aqua vitae"</note>, because it gives life, growth, and progress to all things, and without it, we would be but carrion. Further, it is called Poison; for as long as the Matter lies there raw, it kills everything that eats of it—except for stags and snakes, to whose nature it must be conformed.

1) What kind of care must be exercised during collection?

Answer: Such that nothing metallic is added; that one cleans it of the impurities it has attracted, such as hair, little stones, etc., which can be done either immediately with a clean sliver of wood or with the hands, or afterwards through **filtration** <note>original: *per filtrationem*</note> once it has **dissolved** <note>original: *resolviret*</note>.

2) Whether the vessel in which it is brought home must be sealed?

Answer: A stopper made of paper, or a paper bag, is sufficient; however, one must not expose it to the **sun**, which would release its son from a crystalline prison even before he has entered into **putrefaction** <note>the stage of "rotting" or breakdown necessary for transformation</note> to reach a future better life—if her rays were to reach that far. <note>In alchemical allegory, the "Son" is the volatile spirit within the matter; if exposed to the heat of the sun too early, this spirit would evaporate or "escape" the glass vessel before the chemical process of death and rebirth is complete.</note>

## Fifth Fragment.

The Philosophers <note>original: *Pphen*, an abbreviation for *Philosophen*; in this context, it refers to alchemical authors or "philosophers by fire."</note> always speak of the matter in images, and indeed of one and the same thing perhaps a hundred times in a single book, under a hundred different representations.

...is itself nothing that can be taken from it, for every thing, if it loses something of its **substance** or **essence**, becomes imperfect. What can therefore be taken from it without causing it harm—what one must take from it in order to assist its **essential** effects—that can be nothing other than an **accident** <note>original: *Accidenz*; in the Aristotelian and alchemical tradition, an "accident" is a property (like color or smell) that can change or be removed without changing the core "substance" of the thing itself.</note>. All **bodies** <note>original: *Corpora*</note> in this visible world are, according to their original state, likely nothing but thickened **water** <note>original symbol: ▽, the alchemical element of Water</note>, for in **water** <note>original symbol: ▽</note> everything is dissolved, and every thing willingly mixes with that which is even distantly related to its nature. If one throws a piece of *jelly* <note>original: *Gelée*</note> onto a table strewn with colors, it will take on something from all the colors: red, green, black, yellow, and so forth. Can one then immediately separate the colors from the *jelly* so that nothing remains? No! Rather, one lets the *jelly* turn into water (which happens in a few hours); then one sees all the impurities it has absorbed, and these can best be removed *by filtration* <note>original: *per filtrationem*</note>; they do not belong to the matter, they are *accidental dregs* <note>original: *accidentalia faeces*</note>, a *condemned earth* <note>original: *Terra damnata*; a technical term for the inert, useless solid residue left over after distillation or sublimation</note>, or whatever else one wishes to call it. — But this entire **separation** <note>original: *Scheidung*</note> does not belong to the Work <note>the *Magnum Opus* or the Great Work of alchemy</note>, and one must carefully distinguish it from washing, roasting, boiling, rubbing, and so forth, which is nonetheless nothing other than a thing according to the *Glory of the World* <note>original: *Gloria mundi*; likely referring to a famous alchemical text of that name which discusses the nature of the primary matter</note>.

Common snow-water already does this. <meta>This page continues the discussion on the purification and "nourishment" of the alchemical matter. ...requires more **Water** <note>original: ▽, the alchemical symbol for Water. Given the context of "moistening" and the feminine pronoun "sie" in the German text, this likely refers to the "Earth" or the "Matter" of the work being moistened by its own internal vapors.</note> than it had at the beginning, even if one were to turn the **Work** over a hundred times. For how does **Heaven** relate to the **Earth**? One should direct one's eyes to the great process which the Almighty has prescribed for us in the **Great Book of Nature**. God does not send rain from another planet to moisten our **Earth** <note>original: ▽</note>; rather, that which rises from the Earth itself is sufficient to drench, nourish, and make it **perfect**.

There is nothing of such great scope as the **Ternarius** <note>The "Threefold" or "Ternary," referring to the alchemical trinity of Spirit, Soul, and Body, or the three great hermetic arts.</note>. For one understands by the great branches of this abyss of knowledge: 
a. **Physics** <note>Natural philosophy or the study of the physical world.</note>, 
b. **Kabbalah** <note>The study of divine mysteries and the hidden structure of creation.</note>, 
c. **Magic**. 

1) **The Preliminary Work**: This we leave to God, who has already prepared the way for us through Nature, in that He has created for us, out of the **4 Elements**, that specific thing as a tangible being which alone is suited for our **Work**. 

2) **The Follow-up Work**: This consists of the *solve et coagula* <note>original: *solve und coagula*; a central alchemical maxim meaning "dissolve and coagulate." It refers to the process of breaking a substance down to its core and rebuilding it in a purified state.</note>, that is, in washing, rubbing, cooking, roasting, etc.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of alchemical substances, moving from the definitions of Mercury and Saturn to a specific focus on "Our Antimony." It builds on the previous page's critique of commentators like Kerckring by providing a "true" philosophical definition of the matter.</meta>

...to be reckoned as **Antimony** (♁) <note>symbol: ♁; while often representing Earth, in this context it refers specifically to the alchemical mineral Antimony</note>, not common stibnite <note>original: *Spießglas*; the raw mineral ore of antimony, usually antimony sulfide</note>, but our ***Antimonium*** according to its true meaning; not the common, wild, or raw kind, but that which already begins to ripen in the ***Putrefaction*** <note>the stage of alchemical decay or fermentation where the matter turns black and begins to transform</note>. For *anti* <note>original Greek: $\alpha\nu\tau\iota$</note> means "against" or "opposed to," and *monion* <note>original Greek: $\mu\acute{o}\nu\iota\omicron\nu$</note> means "wild, raw, or unripe." Thus, ***Antimonium*** is that which stands against the wild and the unripe.

It possesses this name as much for its outward appearance as for its inward quality. For in the first ***Preparation***, it is not unlike **Antimony** (♁) in its outward color. As for its inner quality, however, it coincides completely in this: that in the first ***Preparation***, it is still a completely poisonous and harmful essence; but when it is properly and further worked through, it then becomes medicine.

It is well known that common stibnite has much **Mercury** (☿) and **Sulfur** (🜍), from whose impurity the entire "disease" <note>in alchemy, "disease" refers to the impurities or "leprosies" of a metal that prevent it from being gold or medicine</note> of this mineral arises. Our **Antimony** (♁) also has this, consisting of **Sulfur** (🜍) and **Mercury** (☿). But just as the former has four qualities—cold, hot, moist, and dry—so also does this one, and it is governed according to the four seasons. For through its extracted ***Spiritus*** <note>the volatile "spirit" or essence of the substance</note>—that is, through the clear **Mercury** (☿) drawn out of the ***Antimonio***—the **Sulfur** (🜍) is cleansed and purified. This [spirit] is the ***Acetum Antimonii*** <term>Vinegar of Antimony</term> <note>a potent alchemical solvent produced from antimony</note>, by which the black, dark-gray ***Antimonium*** alone is healed, and the white, shining form of the **Mercury** (☿) is transformed <note>original: *verkehrt*; implies a change in state or nature</note>.

*See: Basil Valentine, The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony.* <note>Reference to *Currus Triumphalis Antimonii*, a foundational alchemical text first published in 1604, attributed to the legendary monk Basil Valentine.</note>

Amen!

## Ninth Fragment.

Praised be God the Father of Light, the Angel of the Covenant, of the eternal Word, and the seven-fold powerful **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> from eternity to eternity, Amen!

The first thing to be done is, beyond dispute, the **Purification**. The creation of **Water** (🜄) <note>symbol: 🜄; the element of Water</note> follows thereafter, as **Thales** <note>Thales of Miletus (c. 624–546 BC), a Greek philosopher who famously proposed that water was the "first principle" or originating substance of the universe</note> also says, that **Water** (🜄) is the first element. And from this **Water** (🜄) God first created **Water** <note>The OCR shows the symbol for Water (🜄) again, though contextually this often refers to the separation of the "First Matter" into its active and passive components</note> and **Fire** (🜂), <note>symbol: 🜂; the element of Fire</note> by which the **Fire** (🜂) was turned downwards and the **Water** (🜄) upwards; this is otherwise known as **Sublimation**. <term>Sublimation: In alchemy, the process of heating a substance so it rises as vapor and then solidifies again; symbolically, it represents the purification and "lifting" of the soul or matter</term> And when the **Water** (🜄) is moistened with the **Water** or **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> that has proceeded from it—that is, when it is distilled down upon it—it becomes fruitful and brings forth all kinds of colors; that is, it becomes **perfect** and **more-than-perfect** <note>original: "plusquamperfect"; a term used to describe a substance that has reached a state of extreme potency or multiplication</note>. There is no more to be said regarding the whole of this being. — Now, a few words regarding **Fire** (🜂) for greater clarity. It is true that the **Air** (🜁) <note>symbol: 🜁; the element of Air</note> is a remarkable element: for within it is the living **Seed** of all creatures. But the **Air** (🜁) is not the **Womb** <note>original: "Gebährmutter"</note>, just as a man is not the womb, even though the seed is within him; rather, that is the woman into whom the seed is cast. Thus, the **Air** (🜁) is the man, but the **Womb** is the **Earth** (🜃). <note>symbol: 🜃; the element of Earth</note> There is a **magnetic force** in the **Air** (🜁), through the operation of the element **Fire** (🜂), and it therefore gladly draws all seeds back to itself, if it is not [held] within the womb,

The matter should rather—that is, sooner—hasten toward cold than toward warmth. One first places his **astral salt body** <term>astralisches Salz Corpus: a "body" or substance believed to be impregnated with celestial or "astral" energy</term> into a moist, cold chamber, in order to let it dissolve into **Water** (▽) <note>symbol: ▽; while used for Earth on the previous page, the context of "dissolving" here indicates Water or a liquid state</note>, which is the first **Gordian knot** <note>original: "nodus gordius"; an idiom for a complex or seemingly unsolvable problem</note>. 

But once this **solution** <note>the process of turning a solid into a liquid</note> has occurred, then one takes the **Fire** (△) <note>symbol: △; the element of Fire</note> in hand. None other than the high **Fire** (△) of **Jupiter** (♃) <note>symbol: ♃; Jupiter's fire refers to a specific grade of alchemical heat, often intense and persistent</note>, of which **Abraham Eleazar** <note>a pseudonymous author of famous 18th-century alchemical texts, allegedly teaching ancient Jewish secrets</note> writes: "This alone can burn the wings off the **volatile Mercury** <note>original: "flüchtigen Mercurio"; a metaphor for "fixing" a substance so it no longer evaporates or escapes during heating</note>, so that he can no longer escape." 

That the true **Fire** (△) is to be sought in the **Earth** (▽) <note>symbol: ▽; here the symbol returns to its meaning as Earth, consistent with the previous page</note> is so certain that it is even said of the **Sun** (☉) <note>symbol: ☉; representing Gold or the solar principle</note> in the **Consecration** <note>likely referring to a specific alchemical prayer or ritual text</note>. One places his glass into a **sealing-bath** (L) <note>original: "L"; likely *Lutum*, a protective layer of clay or a sand-bath</note>, in the **Earth** (▽), where it is removed from the air and sun. The fine, fiery **rays** <note>original: "Radii"</note> of the [terrestrial] **Fire** (△) and the upper [celestial] **Fire** (△) work sufficiently within the **Earth** (▽), through the union of the **Astral** with the **Central** [forces], such that one must not open gate or door. 

For all **Water** (▽) <note>symbol: ▽; here meaning Water</note> on the whole **Earth** (▽) <note>symbol: ▽; here meaning Earth</note> is by nature the same, like sea-water; but because the rivers pass through the passages of the **Earth** (▽) and are thereby, as it were, "cooked," their salty essence remains in the **Earth** (▽) and they become sweet. There must therefore be a power of the **central Fire** (△) within the **Earth** (▽) after all, for as even common **slovenly chemistry** <note>original: "Sudel-Chemie"; a derogatory term for crude or non-esoteric laboratory work</note> says: "No extraction of salt from the **Earth** (▽) occurs except through **Fire** (△)." 

It remains thus: lead your dissolved **star-matter** (✳) <note>symbol: ✳; referring back to the "Astral Salt"</note> into your **vent-hole** <note>original: "Xloch"; likely a *Kreuzloch*, a specific opening or ventilation point in an alchemical furnace</note> and **Jupiter's** (♃) high **Fire** (△) is sufficient.

### Tenth Fragment.

There is a twofold **Fire** (△) to be taken into consideration: an internal and an external one. The internal one is in the **subject** <note>indicated by the symbol *; likely referring to the "Materia" or the specific substance being worked upon</note> and is nothing other than what the **philosophers** <term>Pphen: abbreviation for "Philosophen" (Philosophers); the historical authorities on alchemy</term> are accustomed to calling the **watery Fire** (△) or the **fiery Water** (▽) <note>Alchemical symbol for Water</note>. To put it more briefly: it is the **spirit** which is taken from the **Water** (▽) and given back to it through **infusion** <note>original: "Aufgießen"; the process of pouring liquid over a solid to saturate or revive it</note>. From this, one can explain why some speak of "baptism with the Spirit" and "baptism with **Fire** (△)."

The external **Fire** (△), however, is to be sought nowhere else than in the place, or **laboratory** <note>abbreviated as "L" in the text</note>, or chamber, or cavern, of which much has already been said. However, it must not be a **laboratory** (L) that is only as large as the glass vessel itself; rather, one should seek a place spacious enough that one can observe the changes occurring within the work. **Welling** <note>Georg von Welling (1655–1727), author of the influential "Opus Mago-Cabbalisticum," a text that merged alchemy, mining, and theology</note> says, therefore: "a dark, clean, and pleasant"—that is, a **spacious**—place. The effects of the **Astral and Central Fire** (△) upon this place were written about in the previous fragment. This is otherwise a matter that is kept very hidden in all the **philosophers'** (Pphen) writings...

### Twelfth Fragment.

By the **Small Stone** <note>original Latin: "Lapide parvo"; a lesser version of the Alchemical Stone</note>, one understands only that which is otherwise usually called **Specification**. The **Small Stone** <note>original Latin: "Lapis parvus"</note>, however, is specific or particular, whether it is applied to metals, or stones, or glass, or plant matter. Once this has happened, it has already been led to its goal or destination where nature guides it, and one cannot lead it back from there or apply it to something else: just as little as one can turn what is intended to be a carnation into an apple tree. On the other hand, the **universal** or **great stone** <note>original Latin: "lapis universalis" or "magnus"</note> can—even if it were brought to its highest perfection—always be determined toward one thing or another, unless it has already matured into the spiritual **Fire** <term>$\triangle$</term> essence or the spiritual magnet; at that point, it is no longer possible for it to be conjoined with anything physical and to proceed further into **putrefaction** <term>the stage of decay or decomposition necessary for alchemical rebirth</term> with it. This decay must necessarily happen, as no generation and fertilization can occur in all of nature except through **putrefaction**.

Now, if **Gold** <term>$\odot$</term> and **Silver** <term>$\leftmoon$</term> are to melt and be dissolved in our **Aqua Fortis** <term>$\nabla$forti; literally "strong water," usually nitric acid</term> or **Aqua Regia** <term>$\nabla$-regis; "royal water," a mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold</term> (that is, water from the **Spring** <note>original: "Fontina"</note> of Count Bernhard <note>Bernhard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist whose works often used the allegory of a fountain or spring</note>), then it is indeed not necessary to have completely worked through the entire process, because then there is a concern that not enough **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term> remains. May God preserve this spark of His knowledge within us; may He increase it, and through it allow us to recognize Him in all His glory. And as we contemplate the things that surround us, let us cast joyful gazes toward the time of our own **clarification** <note>spiritual purification or transfiguration</note> and glorification, as well as that of all creatures.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the preparation and handling of the alchemical "Water" or solvent. ## Sixteenth Fragment.

Concerning the storage of the **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term>, one does not necessarily need to be overly careful with it. After all, rain does not evaporate away while in the clouds. And should this **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term> want to fly away—O! certainly—even the strongest glass, even if one were to fuse it shut <note>referring to hermetic sealing by melting the glass neck of a flask</note>, would not prevent it. Just try placing the raw and not yet **putrefied** <term>putrificirte; decomposed to its base state</term> **Spirit** <term>$\ast$; likely the volatile "Star" or anima of the matter</term> in a very tightly sealed vessel in the sun; it will escape nonetheless, even if it has to shatter everything in its path. 

But once it has "died" <note>reached the stage of mortification or chemical "fixation" where volatility is lost</note>, it will no longer evaporate; consequently, one need not fear losing anything if it is not sealed with extreme force. However, one would only disturb the work—that is, displace something—if it were placed open in the sun; therefore, at most, put a stopper in it and cover it with a cone <note>original German: "Tute"; a paper cap or hood used to protect the contents from dust or direct light</note> of gray paper; that will be sufficient. 

The stopper also should not be pressed in so tightly that one would first need a corkscrew to pull it out, but rather as loosely as possible. Furthermore, the vessel for the **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term> must be equal in size to the one in which the **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term> remains <note>referring to the proportions between the receiving vessel and the original flask</note>, so as not to make the measurement difficult, of which Gualdo <note>likely Federico Gualdi, a mysterious 17th-century alchemist rumored to have discovered the philosopher's stone</note> writes: — The **Final Matter** <term>$\Omega$; the "Omega" or end-product</term> will always have enough space if its container is just as large as the one in which the **Water** <term>$\nabla$</term> lies.

<summary>This fragment provides practical advice on storing alchemical substances, emphasizing that once the matter is "fixed" through putrefaction, it loses its volatile tendency to escape its vessel. It also cites the alchemist Gualdo regarding the appropriate size of laboratory glassware for the final stages of the work.</summary>
<keywords>storage, volatility, putrefaction, fixation, Federico Gualdi, alchemical vessels, spirit, water</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the previous practical instructions regarding the "Water" and "Spirit" to a more philosophical and eschatological discussion. The author links the possession of alchemical secrets to the coming of a spiritual "kingdom of peace" and contrasts the true knowledge of the "Order" with the empty ceremonies of the established church.</meta>

## Seventeenth Fragment.

When the world reaches the point where it knows these truths, the first resurrection will have already occurred, and the visible kingdom of peace will have been established on earth. If it had pleased God, He would not have withdrawn these mysteries from His Church—which truly knew them in the beginning—mysteries of which the Roman Church still possesses the figurative husks <note>original: "Schaalen"; shells or outer coverings without the inner kernel</note> in its ceremonies today, without knowing their true meaning. 

However, that which all antiquity—even the so-called Pagans—entrusted to the hands of the priests, and indeed what the Order received from the altar, so to speak, we must also return to the altar. For the time being <term>pro hunc</term> <note>original Latin: "for this [time]"</note>, it is not for the world, nor for the **Soldier Brothers** <term>Fratres milites</term> <note>likely referring to a specific rank or class within a chivalric or secret order</note>, and even less for princes. It is enough for them if they merely participate in the fruits of the divine essence.

**Note:** The children of the true school always agree with one another, whether one reads the writings of one or the other. The **Sophists** <note>in alchemical literature, "sophists" refers to false practitioners who use logic and trickery rather than true spiritual and natural knowledge</note>, however, never do.

The true **Spirit** <term>$\ast$</term> <note>the asterisk symbol in this context usually represents the volatile "Star" or Spirit of the matter</note> can be distinguished from the false by the fact that the true one is reclaimed by its father, the **Sun** <term>☉</term>, as soon as he appears in the heavens <term>Firmamente</term>, whereas the false one is not.

The 18th and 19th Fragments contain nothing significant.

# Twentieth Fragment.

It is otherwise a principle that to remove the **phlegm** <term>Phlegma</term> <note>the watery, inert residue or "dead" moisture in a substance</note> from a matter, free air is necessary, because no phlegm can be removed without evaporation, and no evaporation occurs without **fire** <term>△</term>, whether it drives up from below or draws from above. However, this is not the case in our work. In the entire **Chaos** <term>✳</term> <note>the original, unformed matter used in the alchemical process</note> there is no phlegm, and if there is indeed a phlegm present, or whatever one chooses to call it, it is removed partly through **putrefaction** <note>the process of decomposition or "rotting" the matter to release its inner life</note> and partly through **imbibition** <note>the process of "feeding" or soaking the dry matter with liquid</note>. —

One may consider our **water** <term>▽</term> to be the seed itself, or the immediate **receptacle** <term>Receptaculum</term> <note>a container or host</note> of the seed; either way, it must go to the external **fire** <term>△</term>, that is, to the **water** <term>▽</term> <note>this refers to the "Water of the Wise," a liquid used to apply heat or dissolve matter</note>. The grain of wheat is not the seed of the future stalk; rather, it is the **germ** <term>Keim</term> that stays hidden inside the grain. The grain, however, is placed into the earth so that it may **putrefy**, etc. The expression that the **Chaos** <term>✳</term> should be subjected to cold rather than heat means, in plain English: place it first in a damp, cool room, or an ordinary room, so that it may **dissolve** <term>solvire</term>, rather than in the heat, for there it **coagulates** <note>thickens or hardens</note>; and the heat must be understood as the dark, subterranean chamber into which it should be brought after the **dissolution** <term>Solution</term> in order to putrefy and separate itself.

The vessel with the **water** <term>▽</term> or **air** <term>〰</term> must not be placed next to the other once the **water** <term>▽</term> has been taken out, but also in the usual—

Do not think that the entire work occurs in the **earth** <term>🜃</term> <note>the element of Earth or the dry, solid matter in the vessel</note>, but only the beginning; afterwards, both can be placed next to each other in a cupboard. — First, then, it is dissolved and purified in the cold, then **putrefied** <note>decomposed or "rotted" to break down its structure</note> or separated in a dark subterranean chamber or cave, then taken up, and **dew** and **rain** <note>alchemical terms for distilled liquids or condensed vapors within the vessel</note> are allowed to fall upon it.

At first, it will look like a jelly, then like water, then it will become thick and **earth** <term>🜃</term> will be at the bottom; and this **earth** <term>🜃</term> will be more or less dry depending on the amount of rain, until finally, after assuming various colors, it will resemble a shimmering salt-body. When one looks at such **gold** <term>☉</term> <note>the solar principle or the refined metallic matter</note> through a magnifying glass, one can clearly recognize the cube-shaped granules. — All of this is to be understood regarding the **Great Stone** <note>original: "de lapide magno"; refers to the highest goal of the work, the Philosopher's Stone</note>.

### Twenty-first Fragment.

When the **philosophers** <term>Pphen</term> <note>short for "Philosophen," the ancient and medieval alchemical authorities</note> use the expression that the **body** <term>Corpus</term> gradually drinks its own **water** <term>🜄</term> until it becomes white, the meaning is this: that one must continue to combine the **water** <term>🜄</term> with the **earth** <term>🜃</term> until it coagulates into a white **salt** <term>🜔</term>, that is, into a solid body called **quicklime** <note>original: "Calx viva"; a caustic, reactive substance, here referring to a chemically "hungry" or active powder</note>, but which is also potentially fluid, if one...

<meta>This page continues the Twenty-first Fragment, specifically discussing the final stages of the metallic solution and the necessity of cold versus heat in the alchemical "turning of the wheel."</meta>

...the last of what remains of the **Water** <term>🜄</term>, however little it may be, is poured in, and then let it be **dissolved** <note>original: "solviren"; the process of turning a solid into a liquid</note> again together in the cold.

Although it cannot be absolutely denied that a **Water** <term>🜄</term> brought to the highest possible degree of perfection could unlock the bonds of **Gold** <term>☉</term> and **Silver** <term>☽</term>—for all fruits placed in **Water** <term>🜄</term> sprout therein, but more slowly and also more sickly, because the **Water** <term>🜄</term> of the **Earth** <term>🜃</term> <note>referring to the moisture within the solid matter</note> has not ignited the spirit of life within them; yet by **Mercury** <term>☿</term> **sublimate** <note>original: "sublimatus"; a substance purified through heating into a vapor and cooling back into a solid</note> one does not always mean **mercurial water** <note>original: "aqua mercurialis"</note>, but **Water** <term>🜄</term> and **Earth** <term>🜃</term> united into one, into a whitish-blue **Solar** <term>☉</term> body, which, however, must necessarily be in a **fluid form** <note>original: "forma fluida"</note> for the **dissolution** <note>original: "solution"</note> of the metals. This **fluid form** <note>original: "forma fluida"</note>, however, is not to be produced: 1) through frequent and superfluous water, for that would cause a **deluge** <note>an alchemical error where too much liquid "drowns" the substance, preventing its transformation</note>, which must be avoided at all costs; 2) nor through a stronger degree of warmth, for the more **Fire** <term>🜂</term>, the more heat, and the more heat, the greater the dryness. Therefore, all "friends" <note>a term for fellow alchemists or initiates into the "Hermetic" arts</note> advise giving more and more **Fire** <term>🜂</term> at the end of the work, and to **coagulate** <note>to thicken or solidify</note> the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> into a firmer and almost glass-like **Salt** <term>🜔</term> essence; instead, the **dissolution** <note>original: "Solution"</note> can only happen in the cold. In the cold, it does not begin to solidify, but to become liquid. — By the **turning of the wheel** <note>a metaphorical term for the repetitive cycles of heating/cooling or dissolving/solidifying</note> one must also not understand that if previously the **Sal ammoniac** <term>🜹</term> dissolved in the cold, it now begins in the warmth...

<summary>This page explores the specific conditions required to dissolve metals, arguing that while heat is used to solidify the final product, the actual dissolution into a "fluid form" must occur in the cold to avoid a "deluge."</summary>

--- Page 284 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the alchemical "turning of the wheel," specifically clarifying linguistic misunderstandings regarding the "reduction" of metals and the desired consistency of the final substance.</meta>

to dissolve; rather, by "turning the wheel" one understands nothing other than beginning again from the start what has already been done once, through **dissolve and coagulate** <note>original: "solve et coagula"; the fundamental alchemical cycle of breaking down a substance into liquid and reforming it into a solid to purify it</note>. The body dissolved in the cold [must] not [be brought] to a total **Fire** <term>🜂</term> <note>symbol for fire or intense heat</note>, for the closer it comes to **Perfection**, the less fluid it will become, and almost like an **Oil**. 

— The words **reduction of metals into oil** <note>original: "Reductio Metallorum in Oleum"</note> are not to be understood as meaning the metals must be reduced *into* an oil, but rather **to reduce into something** <note>original: "reducere in aliquid"</note> means: to bring something back into something else. **He brought me back into the house** <note>original: "Reduxit me in domum"</note>; he led me inside the house. However, **to reduce to something** <note>original: "reducere ad aliquid"</note> means to make someone or something into something else again. Likewise, **to restore someone to health** <note>original: "reducere aliquem ad sanitatem"</note> means to make a person healthy again. This is the minor **fallacy** <note>original: "fallacia"; a deceptive argument or linguistic error</note> that many have not paid attention to, and because of it, they have performed all sorts of things with the poor metal—roasting and parching it so that it should yield an oil, etc. 

**Reduction of metals into oil** therefore means nothing other than that one should bring, introduce, or place the metals (which indeed **in truth** <note>original: "re vera"</note> have their origin from the **Universal Spirit** <term>🜀</term> <note>symbolized by an asterisk, referring to the celestial or stellar influence that gives life to all things</note> like all things) back into the **Matter** <note>original: "Materie"</note>. At the same time, the word **Oil** <note>original: "Oleum"</note> indicates that the **fluid form** <note>original: "forma fluida"</note> will not be like a **Water** <term>🜄</term>, but rather more like an oil. An example of the matter: if one were to throw a piece of **rock candy** <note>original: "Kandel-Zucker"</note> into **olive oil** <note>original: "Baum-Oel"; literally "tree oil," a common historical term for olive or high-quality vegetable oil</note> and let it dissolve there in order to cure one's throat or chest.

<summary>This page addresses a common linguistic error in alchemy, clarifying that "reducing metals into oil" means returning them to their primal matter rather than literally turning them into fat. It emphasizes the importance of a viscous "fluid form" over a watery one and warns against excessive heating.</summary>

<keywords>solve et coagula, Reductio Metallorum in Oleum, alchemical symbols, Matter, fluid form, linguistics in alchemy, rock candy analogy</keywords>

If **Gold** <term>☉</term> or **Silver** <term>☽</term> is to be dissolved in our **Salt** <term>🜔</term> or **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term>, it is not strictly necessary for the entire Work to be completed; for if the "wheel" has been turned seven times <note>referring to seven completed cycles of the 'solve et coagula' process</note>, the **Water** <term>▽</term> will certainly produce the desired effect. At this stage, it will achieve the **dissolution** <note>original: "Solution"</note>, because it has been properly **exalted** <note>refined to a higher state of potency</note>. The **Fire** <term>△</term>, however, is exalted or heightened not by itself, but through repeated **dissolution**, **distillation**, and **coagulation** <note>original: "Solution, Destilation und Coagulation"</note>. Through this process, the **phlegm** <note>the watery, inactive, or "dead" byproduct of a distillation</note> is increasingly removed, and finally both **bodies** <note>original: "Corpora"</note>—the **Water** <term>▽</term> as well as the **Salt** <term>🜔</term>—are made more spiritual, and can even be brought into a fine, **spiritual salt-essence**.

During the second dissolution and the separation of the **Water** <term>▽</term> from the **Salt** <term>🜔</term>, both the **Water** and the **Salt** become less in quantity but better in quality; and as the work continues and the **Salt** is exalted, the **Water** is likewise exalted. The **sulfurous spirit** fixes itself within the **Salt**. 

When the wheel has been turned six times—or, when **dissolution**, **division**, **distillation**, and **coagulation** <note>original: "solutio, divisio, destillatio, coagulatio"</note> have been performed repeatedly six times—and one then begins to dissolve the coagulated body for the seventh time, and then divides this solution into its two elements as before (either into **Water** <term>▽</term> and **Salt** <term>🜔</term>, or into **Salt** and **Sulfur** <term>🜍</term>), it is then possible and the right time to dissolve the metals in the **water** <term>🜒</term> <note>the symbol here refers to a sharp solvent or acidic liquid, often called 'philosophical vinegar'</note> or **liquid** <term>〰</term>. This works just as well (though more slowly) as if the metal were placed into the dissolved body without the **Water** and **Salt** having been separated. The whole matter comes down to one thing: dissolve

### Regarding the Processing.

1) One must choose the place where it is set for solution <term>Solution</term> <note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid, a key step in alchemical work</note> such that the temperature around the glass is uniform.
2) One must leave the vessel undisturbed as long as it is meant to stand, because otherwise, by moving it, one disturbs nature in its certain purpose.
3) One must let it stand until it has dissolved <note>original: "solvirt"</note>; it may take as long as it will, and nature will take its course without hindrance.

not. — In the matter, there is nothing that can be taken from it, for every thing, if it loses something of its substance or essence, becomes imperfect; therefore, what can be taken from it without its harm—what one must take from it in order to come to the aid of the essential effects—can be nothing other than an *accident* <term>Accident</term> <note>In historical philosophy and alchemy, an "accident" is a non-essential quality (like color or smell) that can change without the object losing its core identity.</note>. All *bodies* <note>original: *Corpora*</note> in this visible world are likely, according to their circumstances, nothing but thickened water, for in <image-desc>inverted equilateral triangle: alchemical symbol for water</image-desc> everything is dissolved, and every thing gladly mixes with that which is even distantly of its own nature. If you throw a piece of jelly onto a table sprinkled with colors, it will take on something of all colors—red, green, black, yellow, etc. Can you now separate the colors from your jelly right away so that nothing remains of them? No! Instead, you let your jelly become <image-desc>inverted equilateral triangle: alchemical symbol for water</image-desc> (which happens in a few hours); then you see all the impurities it has taken on, and you can better remove them all *through filtration* <note>original: *per filtrationem*</note>; they do not belong to the matter, they are *accidental dregs* <note>original: *accidental feceses*</note>, a *worthless residue* <term>Damned Earth</term> <note>original: *terra damnata*; the chemically exhausted or useless byproduct remaining after an alchemical process.</note>, or whatever else you wish to call it. But this entire separation does not belong to the Work <note>The "Great Work" or *Magnum Opus* of alchemy</note>, and one must carefully distinguish it from what is called washing, boiling, roasting, and rubbing, which is yet nothing other than a thing according to *The Glory of the World* <note>original: *Gloria Mundi*, a prominent alchemical treatise first published in the early 17th century</note>, in the treatise on the Glory of Adam, toward the end, where it says: *Hermes* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational alchemical texts are attributed</note> speaks thus of it.

## Now concerning the second or actual separation.

All things lie under the curse—all the kingdoms of nature, including our own. But by what means are we freed from it? Through death and decay. Will God then throw any part of us away in the resurrection? Where should He throw it? No! These eyes of mine shall see Him; these arms of mine shall embrace my Savior; my body—which is purified in decay and then transfigured with the spirit—will be present there, beautiful and new. *Let the application be made* <note>original: *Fiat applicatio*; a traditional phrase urging the reader to apply this spiritual or theological truth to the practical alchemical work at hand.</note>. — What does the Artist <note>The "Artist" refers to the alchemist practicing the art.</note> have to do in our Work? <term>Sendivog</term> <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and physician known for his influential writings on the "Central Nitre."</note> says in his 6th treatise: The artist does nothing here except that he knows how to separate the subtle (that is, the Spirit <image-desc>inverted equilateral triangle: alchemical symbol for water or, in this context, the Spirit</image-desc>) from the sharp and coarse (that is, the Earth <image-desc>inverted triangle with a horizontal line: alchemical symbol for earth</image-desc>) and to put them into a vessel. For this must be observed: as a thing is begun, so it is also ended. From one become two, and from two comes one. How many are here? Only two, namely <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: *Schwefel*; in alchemy, sulfur often represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle of matter.</note> (the Earth <image-desc>earth symbol</image-desc> and Mercury <image-desc>symbol for the planet/element mercury</image-desc>) and Spirit <image-desc>inverted triangle symbol</image-desc>; *Body and Spirit* <note>original: *Corpus et Spiritus*</note>. But the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Pphi*, an abbreviation for *Philosophi*; here referring to the ancient alchemical authorities.</note> still speak of a third, that is, the Soul <image-desc>circle symbol, likely representing the Sun or the Soul</image-desc>. Where is that? In both, in the Sulfur <image-desc>symbol for sulfur</image-desc> and the Mercury <image-desc>symbol for mercury</image-desc>; that is *Body, Soul, and Spirit* <note>original: *Corpus, Anima, Spiritus*</note>. From one become two, and within the two is three. What follows then, you will find clearly enough stated in the *Opened Philosophical* <term>Father-Heart</term> <note>original: *Vaterherz*; likely referring to the alchemical-mystical text *Das eröffnete philosophische Vaterherz* (The Opened Philosophical Father-Heart), often associated with the circle of Jacob Böhme.</note>, Chapter 8. — One must first know how to do all of this in the head before trying it with the fist <note>Meaning one must understand the theory before attempting the manual laboratory work.</note>. For a thing

...the exit should already have been brought home. When it has been found, one must preserve it carefully so that no metal comes into contact with it, and separate all impurities—such as glass, hair, small stones, and so on—with a wooden spatula or clean hands. This is called the first "solution" <note>The process of dissolving or breaking down a substance.</note>, which does not yet belong to the actual Work, but is known as the external cleaning, which can also be accomplished through filtration.

Afterward, place the entire purified mass (which according to Paracelsus <note>abbreviated: PC</note> is also cleaned by washing) into the designated vessel using a clean wooden spoon. Seal the vessel with a cork stopper and a bladder dipped in oil as tightly as possible, and place the vessel in a cool, damp room where no sun shines upon it and it remains undisturbed. This is done so that the second "solution"—which truly belongs to the Work and through which a vessel becomes Water <note>symbol: ▽</note>—may take place. Note Well: this occurs in a period of 45 days.

When the Salt <note>symbol: ✱</note> has become Fire <note>symbol: △</note>, one takes it and places it into the Christian Life <note>abbreviation: Xte L; likely a specific furnace or a metaphor for a gentle, consistent heat source</note>, so that it may undergo putrefaction <term>Putrefaction: the chemical "rotting" or breakdown of the matter, considered a necessary stage of death before the substance can be "reborn" or purified.</term> and so that Water <note>symbol: ▽</note> may separate from Air <note>symbol: 🜁</note> within 45 days. However, before doing this, remove the oiled bladder, place the cork on very loosely, and cover it only with a paper cone <note>original: Tute</note>.

When the putrefaction is completed and Water and Earth have separated, the Water is poured off slowly—without becoming cloudy—into a vessel identical to the one in which the Water <note>symbol: ▽</note> remains. Both vessels are then taken out of the Christian Life <note>Xten L</note> and placed in a cool room where no...
