...sun comes, firmly set. When the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> has become fairly dry after the mentioned pouring off of Water <note>symbol: ∇</note>, then [the Water] is cautiously, carefully, slowly, and moderately dripped on from the Water <note>symbol: ∇</note> in the manner of rain, so that no Great Flood <note>German: Sündfluth; a warning against over-saturating the matter and "drowning" the chemical reaction</note> arises; this is called "imbibing" <term>Imbibition: the process of a solid substance soaking up a liquid, much like a dry sponge or a plant taking in water to be "nourished."</term>, and this is continued (usually happening every 8 days) as soon as the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> begins to dry, until the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> has drunk all the Water <note>symbol: ∇</note>. During these various imbibitions, one will already see colors appearing, according to letter 9, folio 5, at the beginning, and letter 14, folio 1 <note>original Latin: secundum litter. 9. fol. 5. principio et litter. 14. fol. 1.</note>. When the imbibition is complete, one turns the wheel <note>An alchemical metaphor for repeating a cycle of the process to increase the "potency" of the substance.</note> anew; that is, one places the vessel, in which the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> is found with all the "drunk" Water <note>symbol: ∇</note>, anew into the "Christian Life" <note>Abbreviation: Xte L; as noted on the previous page, this refers to a specific furnace or a gentle heat source such as a water bath or dung bed</note> for putrefaction <term>Putrefaction: a stage of chemical breakdown where the matter "dies" and turns black before its eventual purification and rebirth.</term>, so that Water <note>symbol: ∇</note> and Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> may separate again, which happens in the time of — <note>The author has left a blank space here, possibly for the reader to fill in based on their own results or to keep the timing a secret.</note>. When this has happened, one proceeds as stated above, again by pouring off the Water <note>symbol: ∇</note> from the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note>, and imbibes anew. When this has been repeated six times, or one has "turned the wheel" six times, and one now proceeds to the seventh imbibition, then the colors—black, white, and red—appear. The wheel must not be turned more often than ten times. Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and courtier</note> says in the sixth treatise: it is to be noted that as a thing is started, so it is also ended; from one become two, and finally from two, one.

Gualdo <note>Likely Federico Gualdi, a mysterious 17th-century alchemist rumored to have discovered the elixir of life</note> says: seven times I have [treated/washed] this lion...

...the Sun comes, firmly established. When the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note>, after the aforementioned pouring off of Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, has become quite dry, then the Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note> is carefully, cautiously, slowly, and moderately dripped on—in the manner of rain—so that no Deluge <note>original: "Sündfluth"; a reference to the Great Flood, suggesting the matter should not be overwhelmed by liquid</note> occurs. This is called "imbibing" <term>Imbibition: the process of adding liquid to a dry substance so it can absorb or "drink" it, symbolizing nourishment and growth.</term>, and this is continued as soon as the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> begins to dry (which usually occurs every 8 days) until the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> has drunk all the Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note>. During these various imbibitions, one will already see colors arise, according to Letter 9, Folio 5, at the beginning, and Letter 14, Folio 1 <note>original Latin: "secundum litter. 9. fol. 5. principio et litter. 14. fol. 1."</note>.

When the imbibition is complete, one turns the wheel <term>The Wheel: a metaphor for the repetitive cycles of distillation, evaporation, and condensation in the alchemical vessel.</term> anew; that is, one places the vessel in which the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> and all the "drunk" Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note> are contained, once more into putrefaction <term>Putrefaction: the stage where the matter is decomposed through gentle heat, often resulting in a black color.</term> in Xte L <note>The meaning of "Xte L" is unclear; it likely refers to a specific grade of heat or a digestion vessel such as a sand bath.</note> so that the Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note> and Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note> might separate again, which happens in a time of — <note>space left blank in original</note>. 

When this has happened, one proceeds as stated above: again pouring off the Water <note>symbol: 🜄</note> from the Earth <note>symbol: 🜃</note>, and imbibing anew. Now, when this has been repeated six times, or one has "turned the wheel" six times, and one then proceeds to the seventh imbibition, the colors will then appear: black, white, and red <note>These represent the three major stages of the Great Work: Nigredo, Albedo, and Rubedo.</note>. The wheel must not be turned more than ten times.

Sendivogius <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and physician.</note> says in his sixth treatise: it must be noted that as a thing is begun, so shall it be ended; from one come two, and finally from two, one.

...or the turning of the wheel and the separation of the **Water** <term>symbol: ▽</term> from the **Salt** <term>symbol: 🜇; likely Sal Alkali or common salt</term>, both the **Water** and the **Salt** become less in quantity but better in quality. This continues further because the **Phlegm** <term>Phlegma: the watery, tasteless, and inert residue left after distillation</term> is increasingly lost through the labor; and as the **Earth** is **exalted** <term>Exaltation: a process by which a substance is raised to a higher purity or "dignity"</term> during the work, the **Water** is also exalted.

**To Letter 14, page [b].** Regarding the point in time when one can think of the **Specification** <note>original: "Specification"; the stage where the generic "medicine" is directed toward a specific metal like gold or silver</note>, and how one can undertake the **reduction of metals into mercury** <note>original Latin: "reductionem metallorum in °°"; °° is a shorthand for Mercury or the "First Matter" of metals</note>.

**To Letter 19.** When the purification or the turning of the **Natural Wheel** has occurred seven times, then that stage is reached which produces the **Stone of Health** <note>original Latin: "Lapidem sanitatis"; a medicinal form of the Philosopher's Stone</note>, or also the **Stone of Metals**.

Regarding the various times, see **Gualdo** <note>Girolamo Gualdo, an Italian alchemist</note>, Chapters 27, 28, 40, and 49. **Welling** <note>Georg von Welling (1655–1727), author of "Opus Mago-Cabbalisticum"</note>, page 200.

Regarding the measure of **Imbibition** <term>Imbibition: the process of "feeding" a dry substance with liquid until it is saturated</term>. **Gualdo**, Chapters 34, 12, 18. **A. B. C. L.**, page 208. **A. B. C. 11.**, page 68.

**Letter 4, Section 1.** Primarily, the **Star** <term>symbol: ∗; often refers to Sal Ammoniac or a specific crystalline state</term> that has become a **Water** <term>symbol: ▽</term> can be called **boys' urine** <note>original Latin: "Urina puerorum"; a common alchemical code for a specific mineral solution or salt, rather than literal urine</note>, because it looks somewhat yellowish and, in the first processing, has a similarity to urine in its smell.

**Letter 4, Section 2, B.** That which has become **Water** from the **Star** throws only a small mass to the bottom during **Putrefaction** <term>Putrefaction: the stage of "death" and decomposition of the matter, usually marked by a black color</term>.

**C.** The union can happen **by filtration** <note>original Latin: "per filtrationem"</note> once it has resolved itself, or even immediately when it is found, using the hands or small wood splinters.

**D.** Winter is not a suitable time for gathering; **see Archidoxis, Letter to Sulpicius** <note>Refers to the "Archidoxis" of Paracelsus, a foundational text of iatrochemistry</note>.

---

->End of the first section.<-

It provides citations for the timing of these processes and describes the physical characteristics of the "boys' urine" (a chemical solvent) during its putrefaction.</summary>
